<div class="alpha-section" id="rt-B">
<h3 style="color:#5b8dee;border-bottom:1px solid #5b8dee;padding-bottom:3px;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">B</h3>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="beyond the polish — constructing feminist humor in afiadas izadora xavier do monte (uepb), veronica debom (rede globo), barbara byington duvivier (o2), xaman minillo (ufpb), rebecca a. krefting (skidmore college)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">Beyond the Polish — Constructing Feminist Humor in Afiadas</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Izadora Xavier do Monte</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UEPB)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Veronica Debom</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Rede Globo)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Barbara Byington Duvivier</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(O2)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Xaman Minillo</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFPB)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Rebecca A. Krefting</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Skidmore College)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">This roundtable presents Afiadas, a Brazilian comedy produced by O2 Filmes and broadcast by TV Brasil, as a case study in contemporary feminist humor. Set in a beauty salon—a historically marginalized and feminized workspace—the show transforms the manicurist’s chair into a site of political reflection, interviews, and sharp social critique, reclaiming a space long stereotyped as trivial. Featuring screenwriter Barbara Duvivier and actress Veronica Debom, moderated by sociologist Izadora Xavier do Monte and discussed by Beck Krefting, the panel explores the dramaturgical, political, and production challenges of creating explicitly feminist comedy, asking how humor can redefine political boundaries, expose structures of inequality, and contribute to imagining a joyful, collective feminist future.</p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class="alpha-section" id="rt-F">
<h3 style="color:#5b8dee;border-bottom:1px solid #5b8dee;padding-bottom:3px;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">F</h3>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="the future of humor studies: critical perspectives and new pathways for emerging humor studies collectives and associations rebecca a. krefting (skidmore college), rujuta date (university of michigan), jessyka finley (pomona college), sarah ilott (manchester metropolitan)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">The Future of Humor Studies: Critical Perspectives and New Pathways for Emerging Humor Studies Collectives and Associations</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Rebecca A. Krefting</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Skidmore College)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Rujuta Date</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(University of Michigan)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Jessyka Finley</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Pomona College)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Sarah Ilott</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Manchester Metropolitan)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">This roundtable brings together seven founders and leaders of five emerging humor studies associations from all over the world to discuss how new collectives are shaping the field’s scholarly directions, political commitments, and collaborative possibilities amid global pressures on critical inquiry.</p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class="alpha-section" id="rt-M">
<h3 style="color:#5b8dee;border-bottom:1px solid #5b8dee;padding-bottom:3px;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">M</h3>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="modos de rir, modos de sentir: humor e política no brasil contemporâneo thaís leão vieira (ufmt), julia glaciela da silva oliveira (ufabc), cintia lima crescêncio (ufabc), emerson césar de campos (udesc)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">Modos de Rir, Modos de Sentir: Humor e Política no Brasil Contemporâneo</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Thaís Leão Vieira</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFMT)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Julia Glaciela da Silva Oliveira</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFABC)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Cintia Lima Crescêncio</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFABC)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Emerson César de Campos</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UDESC)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">Esta mesa-redonda propõe examinar como o humor, em suas expressões literárias, gráficas, televisivas, e nas práticas contemporâneas de artivismo feminista, operou como ferramenta de mediação política, crítica social e produção de sentidos durante momentos decisivos da história recente do Brasil. A partir de análises da crônica jornalístico-literária, do humor televisivo de massa, das produções frequentemente marginalizadas de mulheres cartunistas e das estratégias humorísticas acionadas por coletivos feministas em suas intervenções públicas, os trabalhos investigam de que modo riso, ironia e sátira construíram representações políticas, elaboraram traumas coletivos e reconfiguraram sensibilidades públicas em diferentes mídias e contextos sociais. This roundtable proposes to examine how humor, in its literary, graphic, and television expressions, as well as in contemporary feminist artivist practices, has operated as a tool of political mediation, social critique, and meaning-making during decisive moments in Brazil’s recent history. Drawing on analyses of journalistic-literary chronicles, mass television humor, the often marginalized productions of women cartoonists, and the humorous strategies employed by feminist collectives in their public interventions, the papers investigate how laughter, irony, and satire have constructed political representations, worked through collective traumas, and reconfigured public sensibilities across different media and social contexts.</p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class="alpha-section" id="rt-O">
<h3 style="color:#5b8dee;border-bottom:1px solid #5b8dee;padding-bottom:3px;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">O</h3>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="online humor and pop cultures in transcultural contexts adriana da rosa amaral (uff), simone pereira de sá (uff), eloy santos vieira (ufs), julia carneiro abrahão de souza (uerj), joana perci benigno (puc-rio), larissa cardozo pinto de arruda (uff), ana karolina de carvalho pereira araújo (uff)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">Online Humor and pop cultures in Transcultural Contexts</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Adriana da Rosa Amaral</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Simone Pereira de Sá</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Eloy Santos Vieira</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFS)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Julia Carneiro Abrahão de Souza</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UERJ)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Joana Perci Benigno</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(PUC-RIO)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Larissa Cardozo Pinto de Arruda</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Ana Karolina de Carvalho Pereira Araújo</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">This roundtable aims to discuss the role of online humor in the circulation of memes originating in Brazil within a transcultural context. The proposing scholars are members of the Cultpop and Labcult Research Labs, both focused on pop and digital cultures. In this sense, we offer a series of provocations to reflect on the intersections between these themes and the dissemination of online humor across different digital platforms such as Tumblr, BChart and X. From memes to music, pop literature, and fanfiction, humor has taken multiple formats and genres across digital platforms. This roundtable examines and propose questions on how humor and pop culture shape distinct performative practices and contribute to the construction of shared languages of community and sociability in online environments.</p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class="alpha-section" id="rt-P">
<h3 style="color:#5b8dee;border-bottom:1px solid #5b8dee;padding-bottom:3px;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">P</h3>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="the politics of humor in video games and gaming cultures josé messias (uff), fátima cristina regis martins de oliveira (uerj), dara coema (uff), natalia corbello (uff)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">The Politics of Humor in Video Games and Gaming Cultures</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">José Messias</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Fátima Cristina Regis Martins de Oliveira</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UERJ)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Dara Coema</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Natalia Corbello</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">Videogames have lately been at the center of a heated debate concerning the role of playfulness in the radicalization of young people – especially young man – to the far right (Pearce, 2014; Massanari, 2024). Furthermore, the current discussions in the academic field of game studies bring the subject of the colonization of videogames by neoliberal capitalism and the industry alignment with practices that present games not as a product, but as a service. Within this pressing political context, this roundtable proposes to discuss the intersection of games, humor, and politics in contemporary internet discourse, aiming to investigate how videogame poetics and online gaming cultures are politicized through memes, irony, satire etc.</p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class="alpha-section" id="rt-R">
<h3 style="color:#5b8dee;border-bottom:1px solid #5b8dee;padding-bottom:3px;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">R</h3>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="rethinking humour & transgression nicholas howard ferdinand holm (massey university), dick zijp (utrecht university), delia chiaro (university of bologna), diego hoefel de vasconcellos (ufc), jennalee kitching donian (nelson mandela university)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">Rethinking Humour & Transgression</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Nicholas Howard Ferdinand Holm</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Massey University)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Dick Zijp</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(Utrecht University)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Delia Chiaro</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(University of Bologna)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Diego Hoefel de Vasconcellos</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFC)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Jennalee Kitching Donian</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(nelson mandela university)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">While humour has long been considered frivolous and non-serious, in the twenty-first century, it has increasingly taken on a new cultural and political significance. This is especially true of those forms of humour that shock and provoke, needle and disrupt, upset and ridicule. In short, humour that transgresses. However, in an era when humour and comedy have taken centre stage in social and political debates around issues of identity, social justice, and freedom of speech, and when we witness the rise of troll politics and transnational ‘humour scandals’ (Kuipers, 2011), there is a need to reconsider how we make sense of humour's transgression. This roundtable asks its participants to reflect on what it means to think about humour and transgression. What is the nature of humour's transgression and how does it work? Does it have a stable political or social function, or can comic transgression work to different ends? In an era of populism and polycrises, is there a need to rethink the politics and ethics of humour and transgression? Do we need them more than ever, or has comic transgression run out of steam?</p>
</div>
</div>

<div class="abstract-wrap">
<div class="abstract-card" data-search="roundtable: humor and the right diego hoefel de vasconcellos (ufc), joão paulo capelotti (uff), diego hoefel de vasconcellos (ufc), viktor chagas (uff)" data-type="roundtable" style="color:#1a3a5c;background-color:#eaf0fb;border:1px solid #5b8dee;border-radius:6px;padding:1rem;margin-bottom:0.75rem;">
  <span style="background:#5b8dee;color:#fff;display:inline-block;padding:2px 9px;border-radius:4px;font-size:0.72rem;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:0.5px;margin-bottom:0.5rem;text-transform:uppercase;">Roundtable</span>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.35rem;"><strong style="color:#1a1a2e;">Roundtable: Humor and the Right</strong></p>
  <p style="margin-bottom:0.5rem;"><span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Diego Hoefel de Vasconcellos</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFC)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">João Paulo Capelotti</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Diego Hoefel de Vasconcellos</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFC)</span>, <span style="color:#E960B2;font-weight:600;">Viktor Chagas</span>&nbsp;<span style="color:#6c757d;font-weight:400;">(UFF)</span></p>
  <p style="color:#2d2d2d;font-size:0.93rem;margin-bottom:0;">This roundtable brings together scholars working on the intersections of humor and right-wing politics in order to discuss how humor operates not simply as a style or genre, but as a political and cultural practice. Across different national and media contexts, right-wing humor circulates through irony, ridicule, provocation, and the familiar defence of “just joking,” often helping to normalize exclusionary ideologies, sustain symbolic violence, and shape public discourse in ways that exceed the comic text itself. The session is particularly interested in perspectives that move beyond the usual Anglo-American focus and attend to the specific histories, local conditions, media ecosystems, and political tensions through which right-wing humor takes shape. By bringing together scholars working on different contexts, the roundtable aims to examine how humor travels, adapts, and gains force within contemporary right-wing cultures, while also reflecting on the methodological, ethical, and political challenges involved in researching comic forms entangled with racism, white supremacy, ultranationalism, misogyny, and other violent or exclusionary formations.</p>
</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>
